%% u0092:0-39, u0093:0-85 %%
DM: We begin on May 24th in the hot, humid tropical city of Drankor, the air laden with moisture as the sun rises towards noon.

%% u0094:0-41, u0095:0-14, u0096:0-137, u0097:0-112, u0098:0-94 %%
DM: Far to the west, on the plains of Dunmar, it is morning, and the sun rises over burning grasslands, and the sound of thundering hooves. In the dry heat of high summer, fires burn fast and easily. Dunmari struggled to control their mounts in the face of the flames, and the wind seemed to shift treacherously, driving the fire from the steely archers of the Chardonian Legion towards the fleeing Dunmari.

%% u0099:0-117, u0100:0-32, u0101:0-111, u0102:0-53 %%
DM: All is chaos, except a small but growing knot of mounted warriors surrounding a streaming banner, a golden spear on a green background. Sura's banner. Despite the screams and the heat, she holds her troops together as they race ahead of the flames towards Tokra, retreating in the face of the Chardonian war wizards.

%% u0103:0-93, u0104:0-33, u0105:0-63, u0106:0-199 %%
DM: A few 100 miles east, the morning sun catches on a dust cloud well north of the city of Tokra, a sign of a small stoneborn army, small but mighty in martial prowess, marching with great haste. Few Dunmari notice, however, for despite the morning hour, the streets are empty. The only sound, the screams of terror that occasionally rend the air, and the sour, acrid stench of chalyte and death.

%% u2589:0-106, u2590:0-56, u2591:0-91, u2592:0-88, u2593:0-86, u2594:0-46, u2595:0-131, u2596:0-155, u2597:0-56, u2598:0-88, u2599:0-54, u2600:0-79, u2601:0-71, u2602:0-47, u2603:0-30, u2604:0-18 %%
DM: Yeah. She updates you a little. “On the morning before dawn, the Chardonian wizards rained fire on our camps with an evil wind that fanned the flames into an inferno.” They fled, taking shelter along the river where they could. “But sometime shortly before noon”—should have been like 3 or set or 2 in the afternoon here, or something like that, or one—“the most amazing and unexpected thing happened. A woman riding fast on a phantom steed steed chased us down, waving a flag of parley. She spoke urgently, claiming that the commander of the Chardonian legions, General Marius Cusico, had called a halt to hostilities, saying that he had come to realize the gods did not want this war, and he had ordered his legions to retreat back to Chardon and pray for further guidance. Indeed, he said, he actually asked for aid if possible, as a small contingent of his wizards and legions—not many, but enough to cause trouble—had refused to acknowledge his command and fled camp. We agreed to send a scouting party, at least to see if how many there were. But if he's retreating, not going to ask them to stay to deal with whatever small number of wizards and legions may remain. I don't know what you did, but I appreciate that.”
